(Follow Decoder Task section)\n  - How to discretize continuous outputs (Follow Decoder Task section)\n  - Which inputs/outputs should be **time-varying** vs **per-trial** (Follow Decoder Task section)\n  - How does the reference code load and manipulate this data?\n  - When possible, import or copy code from the reference code in `/app/code` directory. \n- Consult CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4 and reference materials to determine:\n  - Temporal binning for spike rates (if applicable)\n  - Temporal alignment of trials\n  - Data filtering and curation rules for neurons, trials, and sessions\n  - Brain regions recorded from\n- Mapping should be consistent with CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4.\n- Check for variables indicating valid data periods — exclude invalid data\n- Note all decisions, edge cases, and data quality issues\n- Plan and document **sanity and consistency checks** based on dataset size information from the reference texts, code, and data\n\n**Done when**:\n- You have a complete mapping document in CONVERSION_NOTES.md specifying every source-to-target variable mapping under \"Step 5\"\n- You have documented all decisions under \"Step 5\"\n- You have documented sanity and consistency checks under \"Step 5\"\n\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 6, update Step 5 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n\n---\n\n### Step 6: Script Development\n\n**Goal**: Write the conversion script to `/app/convert_data.py`.\n\n**Actions**:\n- Write all conversion code in the file `/app/convert_data.py`\n- Script should run as **`python -u /app/convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`**\n- Include options:\n  - `--full` (default): Process all sessions\n  - `--sample`: Process only 2 sessions for testing\n  - `--show-processing`: Plot visualizations of EVERY processing step for up to 2 sessions. Save plots as `processing_<session_id>.png`.\n- Reuse code from reference code bases where appropriate\n- Use `pynwb` to interface with the data. Do not directly load and parse the `hdf5` files with `h5py`.\n- Write efficient code:\n  - Vectorize loops\n  - Avoid unnecessary file I/O\n  - Use parallel processing if beneficial\n  - Print timing information to find bottlenecks\n- Plots for `--show-processing` mode should visually convince the user that every step of the conversion is correct.\n  - There are no temporal misalignments\n  - Discretization of continuous outputs is correct\n- Write clear, well-documented functions\n- Handle missing data appropriately (consult references, use sensible defaults, document)\n- Validate data shapes and types at each step\n- Include sanity checks (e.g., trial counts match across arrays)\n\n**Done when**: `/app/convert_data.py` exists and runs efficiently without errors.\n\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 7 until `/app/convert_data.py` exists and you have updated Step 6 in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n\n---\n\n### Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n\n**Goal**: Validate the conversion on a small sample before processing the full dataset.\n\n**Actions**:\n1. Run `python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing 2>&1 | tee /app/conversion_sample_out.txt`\n  a. Verify the sample data structure matches the specification.\n  b. Check dimensions are consistent across trials/sessions.\n  c. Check that no data is missed during conversion. \n  d. Validate that metadata accurately describes the data.\n2. Improve efficiency of conversion.\n  a. Look at and note timing information for the conversion. \n  b. Estimate how long the full conversion will run. When you do this, make sure that your estimate accounts for differences in lengths/numbers of trials/sessions. \n  c. If full conversion time estimate is longer than 15 minutes, speed up the code by writing more efficient code (vectorizing loops, removing unnecessary or redundant computations) and/or including parallel processing. \n3. Run `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/sample_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee /app/verification_sample_out.txt`.\n4. Analyze the output file `/app/verification_sample_out.txt`:\n  a. Verify no errors reported.\n  b. Attempt to address any warnings\n  c. Check input ranges and output value distributions against expectations from reference texts.\n\n**Done when**: \n- `/app/sample_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n- `/app/verification_sample_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n- Processing plots show no anomalies\n- Document sample statistics in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 7\"\n\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 8 until `/app/sample_data.pkl` and `/app/verification_sample_out.txt` files exist, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 7 is filled in. \n\n---\n\n### Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n\n**Goal**: Verify the decoder runs successfully on sample data.\n\n**Actions**:\n1. Run `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/sample_data.pkl 2>&1 | tee /app/train_decoder_sample_out.txt`\n2. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n3. **Check accuracy**: Verify accuracy is above chance for EVERY output\n\n**Investigate any issues**:\n- If accuracy is low: check for conversion bugs or reconsider output representation\n\n**Done when**: \n- Decoder training completes without errors\n- Loss decreases over epochs\n- Accuracies are above chance\n- Document all results in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 8\"\n\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 9 until `/app/train_decoder_sample_out.txt` file exists, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 8 is filled in. \n\n---\n\n### Step 9: Full Conversion and Validation\n\n**Goal**: Process the complete dataset.\n\n**Actions**:\n1. Review your time estimate from Step 7.\n2. If estimated time is long (e.g. longer than 15 minutes), optimize bottlenecks first.\n3. Run `python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/converted_data.pkl --full 2>&1 | tee /app/conversion_full_out.txt`\n4. As the code runs, update your estimates of how long processing will take. If it is much longer than your previous estimate (> 1.5x), kill the process, optimize bottlenecks, and repeat. \n5. Run `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee /app/verification_full_out.txt`. \n6. Check that no data was lost during conversion \n7. Spot-check a few sessions to verify data integrity\n8. **Check for consistency** between dataset statistics in `/app/verification_full_out.txt` and the reference texts\n9. Investigate any inconsistencies, and revise the conversion script until all dataset statistics are consistent\n\n**Done when**:\n- `/app/converted_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n- `/app/verification_full_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n- **ALL** dataset statistics are consistent with values from reference texts\n- You have documented statistics and consistency in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 9\"\n\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 10 until `/app/converted_data.pkl` and `/app/verification_full_out.txt` files exist and CONVERSION_NOTES.MD Step 9 is filled in.\n\n---\n\n### Step 10: Critical Review 1\n\n**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain.\n\n**Check 1: Output log verification**\nRead through `/app/verification_full_out.txt` to find and fix **all errors**. Address each warning by either fixing its cause or verifying it that it is not possible to fix it. Explain why each warning could not be fixed in `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n\n**Check 2: Construct sanity checks**\n- Develop at least one sanity check each on neural, input, and output data.\n- These must involve loading in the original data files (NOT through your conversion code).\n- Construct comparison tests and criteria using `np.allclose()`. \n- These tests can be spot-checks of the data, e.g. neural activity at trial 5, timepoint 10, neuron 3. \n- Fix all mismatches/sanity check failures. \n- Document your sanity checks to `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n\n**Check 3: Reference code comparison**\n- Compare your code to the reference code and methods. \n- For each major processing step — (a) data loading, (b) neuron/trial filtering, (c) temporal alignment, (d) binning, (e) input construction, (f) output construction — identify the corresponding code in your script and in the reference, and compare the logic. \n- Check whether you use the same variables, filtering, and processing as the reference code. \n- Fix any mismatches. \n- Document your comparisons in `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n- Explain your reasoning for any differences in `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`.\n\n**Check 4: Key statistics comparison**\n- Verify that key statistics (number of animals, sessions, trials, neurons, input and output ranges, output distributions) match information available in the reference papers. \n- Write code to investigate any discrepancies. \n- Fix any discovered issues. \n- Document your comparisons in `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n\n**Check 5: Check for edge cases**\n- Check for off-by-one errors at the beginning/ends of trials, sessions, and subjects, or in definitions of the inputs and outputs. \n- There may be minor issues in the data that your code must handle. Check that your code robustly handles issues in the original data. \n\n**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n\nWrite a report to CONVERSION_NOTES.md describing **every** check you did, to help convince the user that the conversion code works. **Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 10\".\n\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 11 until you have checked for consistency between **every** statistic in the reference texts, code, and data and documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Step 10. \n\n---\n\n### Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n\n**Goal**: Validate the decoder on the **complete** dataset.\n\n**⚠️ IMPORTANT**: This step must complete fully. Do not skip or abbreviate. Do not proceed to Step 12 until this step is completely finished.\n\n**Actions**:\n1. Run `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --plot-samples 2>&1 | tee /app/train_decoder_full_out.txt`. If the GPU does not have sufficient RAM for the network training, use the flag `--cpu` to specify to use the CPU to train. \n2. **Wait for complete execution** — this may take significant time for large datasets\n3. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n4. **Check accuracy**: High accuracy is an indicator that data conversion has been done correctly. Accuracy near chance is a sign that there might be issues in temporal alignment of signals, choice of data streams, or filtering or processing of data. Compare accuracy to expectations based on the papers. \n\n**Done when**: \n1. Full decoder training completes (the script finishes running)\n2. Accuracy results are documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 11\" with a table of accuracies\n\n---\n\n### Step 12: Critical Review 2\n\n**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain. Perform all of the following checks:\n\n**Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis**\n- For each output, compare validation accuracy to chance (1/num_classes)\n- Accuracy below chance indicates a bug, investigate and fix conversion issues to improve this. \n- Accuracy below 1.5x chance may also indicate a bug, investigate whether changes in conversion logic could improve accuracy. \n\n**Check 2: Accuracy comparison to papers**\n- Find every decoding accuracy reported in the reference papers\n- Create a table comparing your accuracy to the papers' accuracy for each variable\n- If your accuracy is lower than the reported accuracy, this may indicate a bug in your conversion that you must fix. \n- Differences in algorithm/architecture should be relatively small because of the data dimensionality and size. Do not use this as an excuse to dismiss differences, investigate them completely before moving on. \n\n**Check 3: Train vs validation gap**\n- If training accuracy is much higher (>1.5x) than validation accuracy for any output, investigate overfitting or data leakage. \n\n**If accuracy is low for an output, try these specific debugging steps:**\n1. Verify the output values are correct by loading raw data and checking 3 specific trials\n2. Check temporal alignment — plot neural + output for a single trial to verify they're synchronized\n3. Check whether the output has enough variation (not 99% one class)\n4. Check that filtering steps for neural activity streams are followed. \n5. Check that processing of the data matches the reference code and papers. \n\n**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n\n**Report all checks and results** to `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md` to convince the user that the conversion and decoding has been done correctly. \n\n**Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 12\".\n\n---\n\n### Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\n\n**Goal**: Finalize documentation and organize files.\n\n**Actions**:\n- Ensure CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete with all:\n  - Key decisions and rationale\n  - Checks for correctness of the conversion\n  - Validation results and tables\n  - Any issues found and how they were resolved\n- Create a user-facing `/app/README.md` summarizing:\n  - Dataset description\n  - How to load and use the converted data\n  - Output format specification\n  - Key statistics\n- Move analysis/investigation scripts to a `/app/cache/` folder\n- Create `/app/cache/README_CACHE.md` documenting cached files\n\n**Done when**: README.md exists, CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete, and directory is clean.\n\n---\n\n## CONVERSION_NOTES.md Template\n\n**Create this file IMMEDIATELY in Step 0. non-str entries (unlabelled sessions) become ''.\"\"\"\n    63\t    return np.array([x if isinstance(x, str) else '' for x in col[:]])\n    64\t\n    65\t\n    66\tdef _bin_mean(idx, y, n):\n    67\t    \"\"\"Mean of y per bin given a per-sample bin index. NaN where a bin is empty.\"\"\"\n    68\t    ok = ~np.isnan(y)\n    69\t    tot = np.bincount(idx[ok], weights=y[ok], minlength=n)\n    70\t    cnt = np.bincount(idx[ok], minlength=n)\n    71\t    with np.errstate(invalid='ignore'):\n    72\t        return np.where(cnt > 0, tot / np.maximum(cnt, 1), np.nan)\n    73\t\n    74\t\n    75\tdef tongue_output(nwb, go):\n    76\t    \"\"\"Discretise tongue y into (n_trials, N_BINS) classes.\"\"\"\n    77\t    ts = nwb.acquisition['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series['Camera0_side_TongueTracking']\n    78\t    t_cam = np.asarray(ts.timestamps)\n    79\t    data = ts.data[:]                        # (n_frames, 3) = x, y, likelihood\n    80\t    y = data[:, 1].copy()\n    81\t    y[data[:, 2] < TONGUE_CONF] = np.nan     # keep only visible frames\n    82\t\n    83\t    # bin the whole session on one grid, then take percentiles of the bin means\n    84\t    gidx = np.floor((t_cam - t_cam[0]) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n    85\t    edges = np.nanpercentile(_bin_mean(gidx, y, int(gidx[-1]) + 1), TONGUE_PCT)\n    86\t\n    87\t    # per trial, bin the same way and digitise against those edges\n    88\t    cls = np.full((len(go), N_BINS), TONGUE_HIDDEN, np.int8)\n    89\t    lo = np.searchsorted(t_cam, go + T_START, 'left')\n    90\t    hi = np.searchsorted(t_cam, go + T_STOP, 'left')\n    91\t    for i in range(len(go)):\n    92\t        b = np.floor((t_cam[lo[i]:hi[i]] - (go[i] + T_START)) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n    93\t        np.clip(b, 0, N_BINS - 1, out=b)\n    94\t        m = _bin_mean(b, y[lo[i]:hi[i]], N_BINS)\n    95\t        ok = ~np.isnan(m)\n    96\t        cls[i, ok] = np.digitize(m[ok], edges)\n    97\t    return cls\n    98\t\n    99\t\n   100\tdef process_session(path):\n   101\t    \"\"\"Convert one NWB file. Returns a dict, or None if the session is dropped.\"\"\"\n   102\t    with NWBHDF5IO(path, mode='r', load_namespaces=True) as io:\n   103\t        nwb = io.read()\n   104\t        units = nwb.units\n   105\t        trials = nwb.trials.to_dataframe()\n   106\t        bev = nwb.acquisition['BehavioralEvents'].time_series\n   107\t\n   108\t        # ---- units: QC classifier verdict ----------------------------------\n   109\t        cls = _text(units['classification'])\n   110\t        good = np.flatnonzero(cls == 'good')\n   111\t        if good.size == 0:\n   112\t            return None\n   113\t        regions = [region_label(a) for a in _text(units['anno_name'])[good]]\n   114\t\n   115\t        # all NWB times are session-absolute seconds\n   116\t        go = np.asarray(bev['go_start_times'].timestamps)\n   117\t        assert len(go) == len(trials), f'{len(go)} go cues vs {len(trials)} trials'\n   118\t\n   119\t        # ---- trials filtering ----\n   120\t        # spike data\n   121\t        # obs_intervals gives one [start, stop] per observed trial, which in some\n   122\t        # sessions is fewer than the behavioural trials. \n   123\t        # a behavioural trial is observed if its start time is one of the interval starts\n   124\t        obs = np.asarray(units['obs_intervals'][good[0]])        \n   125\t        keep = np.isin(trials['start_time'].values, obs[:, 0])\n   126\t        assert keep.sum() == len(obs), f'{keep.sum()} matched vs {len(obs)} observed'\n   127\t\n   128\t        # filter no water trials\n   129\t        keep &= trials['free_water'].values == 0\n   130\t        \n   131\t        # a sessioon has to have at least 2 trials according to the instruction\n   132\t        if keep.sum() < 2:\n   133\t            return None\n   134\t        \n   135\t        trials = trials[keep]\n   136\t        go = go[keep]\n   137\t        n_trials = len(go)\n   138\t\n   139\t        # last tone onset before the go cue; an early lick replays the sample epoch\n   140\t        sample = np.asarray(bev['sample_start_times'].timestamps)\n   141\t        tone = sample[np.searchsorted(sample, go, side='left') - 1]\n   142\t\n   143\t        # ---- inputs --------------------------------------------------------\n   144\t        # bins sit around the go cue, values are seconds since the tone\n   145\t        time_from_tone = CENTERS[None, :] + (go - tone)[:, None]\n   146\t\n   147\t        # photostim onsets are stored as strings relative to trial start\n   148\t        on_s = trials['photostim_onset'].values\n   149\t        has = on_s != 'N/A'\n   150\t        stim_on = np.full(n_trials, np.nan)\n   151\t        stim_on[has] = trials['start_time'].values[has] + on_s[has].astype(float) - go[has]\n   152\t        stim_off = np.full(n_trials, np.nan)\n   153\t        stim_off[has] = stim_on[has] + trials['photostim_duration'].values[has].astype(float)\n   154\t        # NaN compares False, so non-stim trials stay 0\n   155\t        photostim = ((CENTERS[None, :] >= stim_on[:, None])\n   156\t                     & (CENTERS[None, :] < stim_off[:, None]))\n   157\t\n   158\t        inp = np.stack([time_from_tone, photostim], axis=1).astype(np.float32)\n   159\t\n   160\t        # ---- outputs -------------------------------------------------------\n   161\t        # choice is not stored; derive it from instruction x outcome\n   162\t        outcome_s = trials['outcome'].values\n   163\t        side = np.array([SIDE_CODE[x] for x in trials['trial_instruction'].values])\n   164\t        choice = np.where(outcome_s == 'ignore', CHOICE_NO_LICK,\n   165\t                          np.where(outcome_s == 'hit', side, 1 - side))\n   166\t\n   167\t        # per-trial values are repeated across bins so all outputs share one array\n   168\t        out = np.empty((n_trials, len(OUTPUT_NAMES), N_BINS), np.int8)\n   169\t        out[:, 0, :] = choice[:, None]\n   170\t        out[:, 1, :] = np.array([OUTCOME_CODE[x] for x in outcome_s])[:, None]\n   171\t        out[:, 2, :] = np.array([EARLY_CODE[x] for x in trials['early_lick'].values])[:, None]\n   172\t        out[:, 3, :] = tongue_output(nwb, go)\n   173\t\n   174\t        # ---- neural --------------------------------------------------------\n   175\t        # spike_times is ragged: read the buffer once instead of one read per unit\n   176\t        offs = np.asarray(units['spike_times'].data)\n   177\t        allst = np.asarray(units['spike_times'].target.data)\n   178\t        starts = np.concatenate([[0], offs[:-1]])\n   179\t\n   180\t        edges = (go[:, None] + REL_EDGES[None, :]).ravel()\n   181\t        rates = np.empty((good.size, n_trials, N_BINS), np.float32)\n   182\t        for r, u in enumerate(good):\n   183\t            s = allst[starts[u]:offs[u]]\n   184\t            # running spike total at each edge; differencing gives the count per bin\n   185\t            pos = np.searchsorted(s, edges).reshape(n_trials, N_BINS + 1)\n   186\t            rates[r] = np.diff(pos, axis=1)\n   187\t        rates /= BIN                                  # counts -> Hz\n   188\t\n   189\t        return {\n   190\t            'session_id': nwb.identifier,\n   191\t            'subject': nwb.subject.subject_id,\n   192\t            'neural': [np.ascontiguousarray(rates[:, t, :]) for t in range(n_trials)],\n   193\t            'input': [inp[t] for t in range(n_trials)],\n   194\t            'output': [out[t] for t in range(n_trials)],\n   195\t            'regions': regions,\n   196\t            'n_trials': n_trials,\n   197\t            'n_units': int(good.size),\n   198\t        }\n   199\t\n   200\t\n   201\tdef assemble(results):\n   202\t    \"\"\"Stitch per-session results into the target dictionary.\"\"\"\n   203\t    subjects = sorted({r['subject'] for r in results})\n   204\t    sub_ix = {s: i for i, s in enumerate(subjects)}\n   205\t\n   206\t    brain_regions = sorted({g for r in results for g in r['regions']})\n   207\t    reg_ix = {g: i for i, g in enumerate(brain_regions)}\n   208\t\n   209\t    return {\n   210\t        'neural': [r['neural'] for r in results],\n   211\t        'input': [r['input'] for r in results],\n   212\t        'output': [r['output'] for r in results],\n   213\t\n   214\t        'subjects': subjects,\n   215\t        'subject_idx': np.array([sub_ix[r['subject']] for r in results], np.int32),\n   216\t\n   217\t        'brain_regions': brain_regions,\n   218\t        'brain_region_idx': [np.array([reg_ix[g] for g in r['regions']], np.int32)\n   219\t                             for r in results],\n   220\t\n   221\t        'input_names': INPUT_NAMES,\n   222\t        'output_names': OUTPUT_NAMES,\n   223\t        'output_values': OUTPUT_VALUES,\n   224\t\n   225\t        'metadata': {\n   226\t            'task_description':\n   227\t                'Auditory delayed-response task. A 3 kHz or 12 kHz tone (0.65 s '\n   228\t                'sample epoch) instructs a left or right lick, followed by a 1.2 s '\n   229\t                'delay; a 6 kHz go cue opens a 1.5 s response window. Licking '\n   230\t                'during sample or delay replays that epoch. NaN where a bin is empty.\"\"\"\n    68\t    ok = ~np.isnan(y)\n    69\t    tot = np.bincount(idx[ok], weights=y[ok], minlength=n)\n    70\t    cnt = np.bincount(idx[ok], minlength=n)\n    71\t    with np.errstate(invalid='ignore'):\n    72\t        return np.where(cnt > 0, tot / np.maximum(cnt, 1), np.nan)\n    73\t\n    74\t\n    75\tdef tongue_output(nwb, go):\n    76\t    \"\"\"Discretise tongue y into (n_trials, N_BINS) classes.\"\"\"\n    77\t    ts = nwb.acquisition['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series['Camera0_side_TongueTracking']\n    78\t    t_cam = np.asarray(ts.timestamps)\n    79\t    data = ts.data[:]                        # (n_frames, 3) = x, y, likelihood\n    80\t    y = data[:, 1].copy()\n    81\t    y[data[:, 2] < TONGUE_CONF] = np.nan     # keep only visible frames\n    82\t\n    83\t    # bin the whole session on one grid, then take percentiles of the bin means\n    84\t    gidx = np.floor((t_cam - t_cam[0]) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n    85\t    edges = np.nanpercentile(_bin_mean(gidx, y, int(gidx[-1]) + 1), TONGUE_PCT)\n    86\t\n    87\t    # per trial, bin the same way and digitise against those edges\n    88\t    cls = np.full((len(go), N_BINS), TONGUE_HIDDEN, np.int8)\n    89\t    lo = np.searchsorted(t_cam, go + T_START, 'left')\n    90\t    hi = np.searchsorted(t_cam, go + T_STOP, 'left')\n    91\t    for i in range(len(go)):\n    92\t        b = np.floor((t_cam[lo[i]:hi[i]] - (go[i] + T_START)) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n    93\t        np.clip(b, 0, N_BINS - 1, out=b)\n    94\t        m = _bin_mean(b, y[lo[i]:hi[i]], N_BINS)\n    95\t        ok = ~np.isnan(m)\n    96\t        cls[i, ok] = np.digitize(m[ok], edges)\n    97\t    return cls\n    98\t\n    99\t\n   100\tdef process_session(path):\n   101\t    \"\"\"Convert one NWB file. the batch driver uses `qc_mode='classifier'`. |\n| `process_all_sessions` driver | `Sherlock/preprocess_all_ephys.py` | PROCESSING | Reference method-paper parameters: 40-ms window, 3.4-ms stride, -3 to +3 s, classifier QC. |\n| preprocessing helpers | `VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Trial splitting/shuffling and helper transforms used by downstream analyses. |\n\n### Notes\n- Repository is the analysis code for *Brain-wide analysis reveals movement encoding structured across and within brain areas* and uses the MAP dataset (DANDI 000363).\n- Data are electrophysiology, so delta-F/F is not applicable.\n- The exported spike arrays are explicitly documented in code as already aligned to go cue (`go cue time is 0`). The reference preprocessing truncates those relative times before histogramming.\n- Reference method-paper preprocessing used 40-ms sliding windows at 3.4-ms stride over -3 to +3 s. The present decoder specification overrides this with non-overlapping 50-ms bins over -2.5 to +1.5 s.\n- The batch preprocessing driver selects classifier-based unit QC. This is the primary neuron-curation rule to reproduce from NWB quality-control columns, subject to confirming the NWB schema and QC white paper.\n- Session output includes `early_lick_trials`, auto-learn/auto-water/free-water indicators, lick directions/times, go-cue time, correctness, delay/sample periods, stimulation `[laser_power, stim_type, laser_on_time, laser_off_time]`, trial type, CCF labels/coordinates, and per-unit QC.\n- Reference figure analyses often define regular trials by excluding early-lick, auto/free-water, and stimulation trials. Those exclusions are analysis-specific: this task explicitly requires decoding early lick and representing photostimulation, so such trials must not be removed merely for belonging to those classes. Invalid/missing-data exclusions will be decided after NWB inspection.\n- Archived code was treated as non-authoritative because README labels it old/unused/potentially broken.\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n- `/app/data` contains 174 NWB files nested in dataset subdirectories; all access and inspection used `pynwb.NWBHDF5IO` with loaded namespaces (never `h5py`).\n- Each NWB represents one electrophysiology/behavior session. Subject identifiers are in `nwbfile.subject.subject_id`; identifiers and filenames encode session dates.\n- `nwbfile.trials` has 14 columns: start/stop time, trial index/UID, photostimulation onset/power/duration, task/protocol, instruction, early lick, outcome, auto-water, and free-water.\n- `nwbfile.units` contains spike times, waveform/spike-sorting metrics, `unit_quality`, classifier `classification`, Allen CCF `anno_name`, electrode linkage, and per-unit `is_good_trials` masks.\n- `BehavioralEvents` contains absolute timestamps for go, sample, delay, left/right licks, photostimulation start/stop, and trial end.\n- `BehavioralTimeSeries` contains side-camera jaw, nose, and tongue x/y/likelihood traces, normally at 3.4-ms sampling. Some sessions additionally contain lick-port tracking or a second camera.\n- Go-cue checks in three sessions found exactly one go cue per trial, all within trial bounds. Choice reconstructed from instruction/outcome agreed 100% with first post-go left/right lick: hit=instructed side, miss=opposite side, ignore=no lick.\n- Tongue confidence is strongly bimodal (example session: about 10.5% of frames above 0.9); x/y are unreliable when confidence is near zero. Exact visibility threshold remains to be confirmed from methods/reference processing.\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| NWB files / sessions | 174 |\n| Subjects | 28 |\n| Sessions / subject | 3-10 (mean 6.21) |\n| Trials (total) | 94,990 |\n| Trials / session | 264-800 (median 534; mean 545.92) |\n| Raw sorted units (total) | 272,227 |\n| Raw units / session | 493-3,191 (median 1,571.5; mean 1,564.52) |\n| Classifier-good units (total) | 69,453 |\n| Classifier-good units / session | 0-923 (median 390; mean 399.16) |\n| Anatomical labels among classifier-good units | 293 unique; all 69,453 good units labeled |\n| Photostimulation trials | 18,588 across 168 sessions |\n\n### Native Value Distributions\n- Task: all 94,990 trials are `audio delay`, protocol 1.\n- Instruction: right 48,913; left 46,077.\n- Early lick: no early 84,185; early 10,805.\n- Outcome: ignore 14,095; miss 15,641; hit 65,254.\n- Auto-water: 1,339 trials; free-water: 2,450 trials.\n- Unit `classification`: good 69,453; unlabelled 200,922; missing/NaN 1,852.\n- Unit `unit_quality`: good 154,948; multi 117,279. This differs from classifier QC; reference batch code explicitly uses classifier QC.\n\n### Data-quality observations\n- One session has zero classifier-good units and therefore cannot contribute a neural decoder session.\n- `is_good_trials` has one Boolean per trial per unit. Among classifier-good units, 565 units in four sessions contain invalid periods: 64,612 invalid unit-trial entries out of 37,678,473. The fixed-neuron target representation requires a documented policy after consulting QC references.\n- All sessions contain at least 264 trials, so the minimum-two-trial requirement is not limiting after reasonable curation.\n\n---\n\n## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Expected Statistics (from papers/methods)\n| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| Good units | 69,943 | Data paper/QC white paper: “Overall, the dataset consisted of 69,943 good units…” |\n| Behavioral sessions | 173 | “…recorded across 173 behavioral sessions…” |\n| Probe insertions | 655 | “…from which 655 probe insertions were made.” |\n| Fraction of Kilosort2 clusters retained | 25.9% | “This corresponds to 25.9% of clusters reported by Kilosort2.” |\n| Subjects | 28 in supplied NWB data | Native-data count; papers describe the MAP cohort and task subsets. |\n| Neural preprocessing in method paper | 40-ms sliding histogram, 3.4-ms stride, -3 to +3 s | Reference batch driver and preprocessing code. |\n| Video sampling | 3.4 ms/frame (~294 Hz) | NWB timestamps and method-paper processing. |\n| Bilateral ALM photoinhibition performance | 83.2% control to 71.7% stimulation | methods.txt, 17 mice / 93 sessions. |\n| Video choice decoding after go | AUC 0.99 ± 0.01 across 106 sessions | Method paper, mean AUC ROC over second half of response epoch. |\n| Choice-selective single-neuron threshold | AUC > 0.65 | Method paper nested fivefold logistic-regression analysis. |\n\n### Processing Details\n- Mice performed an auditory delayed-response task. Go cue marks response-epoch onset; left/right licking reports choice.\n- Spike times in the authors' exported analysis structures are relative to go cue. The supplied NWBs instead store absolute spike/event timestamps; conversion must subtract each trial's `go_start_times` timestamp.\n- Method-paper ephys preprocessing uses a 40-ms sliding spike histogram with 3.4-ms stride over -3 to +3 s. The decoder task explicitly requires non-overlapping 50-ms bins from -2.5 to +1.5 s, which overrides those analysis-specific parameters.\n- ALM photoinhibition occurs during the last 0.5 s of delay and ends before go cue. The supplied event start/stop timestamps are therefore the authoritative source for the required time-varying photostimulation input.\n- Video marker traces were obtained with DeepLabCut-style x/y/likelihood outputs. Likelihood is strongly bimodal in the NWBs. A confidence threshold is necessary before tongue y discretization; the exact threshold is not stated clearly in the supplied prose/code, so a conventional 0.9 threshold will be evaluated during mapping and visual validation.\n- The method paper commonly analyzes “regular” trials excluding early lick, auto/free-water, and stimulation when estimating unperturbed movement encoding. This decoder task explicitly requires early lick and photostimulation, so those valid classes must be retained.\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n- Kilosort2 clusters were characterized with 15 quality metrics.\n- Manual labels (`good`/`unlabelled`) from 28 penetrations trained five region-specific logistic-regression classifiers (cortex, striatum, thalamus, midbrain, medulla). The resulting classifier `good` units were used in the paper.\n- Reported classifier false-alarm rates: cortex 7.8%, striatum 6.4%, thalamus 7.3%, midbrain 5.5%, medulla 4.3%.\n- The QC white paper explicitly warns that thresholding individual metrics causes unacceptable misses/false alarms; therefore NWB `classification == good`, not `unit_quality`, is the reference-consistent filter.\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Trial exclusions in individual reference analyses are analysis-specific. For this task, trials with required labels and valid go cues are retained, including early-lick, photostimulation, auto-water, and free-water trials, unless neural/video validity prevents representation.\n- `is_good_trials` is a per-unit validity mask and must be respected. A fixed-neuron session matrix favors dropping classifier-good units that are invalid on any retained trial rather than silently filling neural data.\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n|------------------|----------|\n| Choice from behavior video, post-go | AUC ROC 0.99 ± 0.01 (106 sessions; not directly comparable to required neural decoder) |\n| Single-neuron choice selectivity | AUC > 0.65 used as classification threshold, not population decoder accuracy |\n\n### Paper/data discrepancy\n- Papers/white paper report 69,943 good units across 173 sessions. Supplied NWBs contain 69,453 `classification == good` units across 174 files, one of which has zero good units. Excluding that file reconciles the 173-session count but leaves a 490-unit difference, most plausibly a released-data/version or manuscript transposition difference. The conversion will use the supplied NWB classifier labels as authoritative and report the discrepancy rather than inventing units.\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Spike time reference | Author export says spikes already relative to go | NWB spikes/events are absolute session time | Analyses align to task epochs/go | Subtract each NWB trial go timestamp before binning, equivalently histogram absolute spikes with go-shifted edges. |\n| Neural binning | 40-ms sliding windows, 3.4-ms stride, -3 to +3 s | Raw spike times available | Method-paper processing matches code | Decoder specification overrides with 50-ms non-overlapping bins from -2.5 to +1.5 s (80 bins). |\n| QC unit count | Classifier mode is used | 69,453 classifier-good units; one file has zero | 69,943 good units / 173 sessions | Use supplied NWB labels. Exclude zero-neuron file, yielding 173 sessions; document unresolved 490-unit release/manuscript discrepancy. |\n| Unit validity | `is_good_trials` loaded with units | 565 good units have invalid periods in four sessions | Drift-aware QC is part of pipeline | Drop any classifier-good unit not valid on every retained trial; leaves 68,888 units and fixed neuron dimensions without imputation. |\n| Trial exclusions | Some figures use only regular, nonstim trials | Early-lick, water, and stim labels are available | Exclusions depend on analysis | Retain these classes because they are required decoder variables/context; exclude only structurally invalid sessions/trials. |\n| Tone events | Sample events can outnumber completed trials | Latest sample start before each go yields one tone/trial; median tone-to-go 1.85 s | Auditory sample precedes delay/go | Pair each go with latest preceding sample start; variable delays are preserved in elapsed-time input. |\n| Tongue visibility | No clear threshold in reference source | Likelihood is strongly bimodal; 13 sessions have incomplete video coverage | Markers are confidence-scored | Use likelihood >= 0.9 as visible; uncovered or low-confidence bins are class 3. Compute session percentiles only from visible y samples. Threshold choice has negligible impact because confidence is near 0 or 1 and will be plot-validated. |\n| Photostimulation | Stimulation metadata retained | Trial flags differ slightly from event overlap in 13 sessions because some stimulation is outside target window | Inhibition ends before go | Build time-varying input from start/stop event timestamps; a flagged trial may correctly be all-zero inside the requested window. |\n\n### Final cross-source understanding\n- The supplied files are the same MAP electrophysiology dataset used by both papers, with richer NWB-native absolute timing than the authors' preprocessed MATLAB exports.\n- The reference-consistent neural curation is classifier `good`, followed by respecting `is_good_trials`; individual metric thresholds are inappropriate.\n- All requested behavioral categories map directly or deterministically from NWB trial/event fields.\n- Event timestamps, rather than string-formatted trial photostimulation fields, are authoritative for temporal inputs.\n- Every retained trial uses a common go-aligned interval and 80 common 50-ms bins.\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `units.spike_times` | `neural` | For each go cue, histogram selected-unit spikes into edges `go + arange(-2.5,1.5+0.05,0.05)`; divide counts by 0.05 for Hz; transpose to neuron x 80; float32 | `process_one_area`, `sliding_histogram` | Required non-overlapping bins replace reference sliding windows. |\n| `BehavioralEvents/sample_start_times` and go cues | `input[0]` | Pair each go with latest preceding sample start; at each bin center store absolute bin-center time minus tone onset (seconds) | NWB event loading; task epochs | Continuous time-varying elapsed time requested explicitly by Decoder Task. |\n| `photostim_start_times`, `photostim_stop_times` | `input[1]` | Binary 1 when bin center is within any `[start, stop)` interval, else 0 | Reference stimulation output | Uses event timing rather than trial strings. |\n| `trial_instruction`, `outcome`; lick-event validation | `output[0]` choice | ignore -> 2 (no lick); hit -> instructed direction; miss -> opposite direction; left=0, right=1 | Reference `lick_directions` output | Deterministic mapping had 100% agreement with observed first post-go lick in checked sessions. |\n| `trials.outcome` | `output[1]` | ignore=0, miss=1, hit=2 | Reference correctness/lick handling | Per-trial categorical. |\n| `trials.early_lick` | `output[2]` | no early=0, early=1 | Reference `early_lick_trials` | Per-trial categorical; trials retained. |\n| `Camera0_side_TongueTracking` y, likelihood, timestamps | `output[3]` | Resample nearest video frame to each bin center; if no coverage or likelihood<0.9 -> 3; otherwise y below session visible-sample p40 ->0, p40 through p60 inclusive ->1, above p60 ->2 | Marker preprocessing / aligned marker analysis | Session p40/p60 computed over all visible session frames, not separately by trial. Prefer Camera0 for consistency; fall back deterministically to lexicographically first tongue stream only if Camera0 absent. |\n| `subject.subject_id` | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | Sorted unique string IDs and per-session index | NWB metadata | 28 subjects expected. |\n| `units.anno_name` | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | Exact anatomical label strings; globally sorted index | Reference CCF label output | 293 labels before all-trials-valid filtering; no selected unit has blank label. |\n| NWB/session metadata | `metadata.session_info` | File, identifier, subject, raw/retained counts, thresholds and timing notes | — | Enables auditability. |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Session inclusion**: retain every NWB session with at least one classifier-good, all-trials-valid unit and at least two structurally valid trials. The single zero-good session is excluded; expected 173 sessions.\n2. **Neuron QC**: require `classification == \"good\"`; map each unit-local `obs_intervals`/`is_good_trials` vector to behavioral trials and retain their common valid intersection. This preserves all classifier-good units without fabricated values.\n3. **Trial retention**: retain completed trial rows with a corresponding go cue and preceding sample onset. Keep early-lick, photostimulation, auto-water, free-water, hit, miss and ignore trials because required outputs/context depend on them.\n4. **Time convention**: 80 bins with edges [-2.5, 1.5] and centers [-2.475, 1.475] relative to go; metadata offsets describe edges.\n5. **Neural representation**: firing rates in Hz (count / 0.05 s), matching reference `rate=True`; float32 limits memory.\n6. **Tongue visibility**: likelihood >=0.9. Confidence is bimodal, making this robust; missing video coverage maps to the mandated not-visible class rather than dropping otherwise valid neural trials.\n7. **Percentiles**: compute p40/p60 from all confidence-valid y frames across the session, as “over the session” requires. Equality is assigned to middle class.\n8. **Inputs**: elapsed time from tone onset is represented continuously as explicitly required, despite the generic format note suggesting binary onset series for onset-only inputs.\n9. **Output shape**: choice/outcome/early lick are length-3 per-trial integer vectors; tongue y is the fourth output as an 80-point categorical row, so each trial output is represented as a list/array form accepted by validation. Some sessions additionally contain lick-port tracking or a second camera.\n- Go-cue checks in three sessions found exactly one go cue per trial, all within trial bounds. Choice reconstructed from instruction/outcome agreed 100% with first post-go left/right lick: hit=instructed side, miss=opposite side, ignore=no lick.\n- Tongue confidence is strongly bimodal (example session: about 10.5% of frames above 0.9); x/y are unreliable when confidence is near zero. Go cue marks response-epoch onset; left/right licking reports choice.\n- Spike times in the authors' exported analysis structures are relative to go cue. The supplied NWBs instead store absolute spike/event timestamps; conversion must subtract each trial's `go_start_times` timestamp.\n- Method-paper ephys preprocessing uses a 40-ms sliding spike histogram with 3.4-ms stride over -3 to +3 s. The decoder task explicitly requires non-overlapping 50-ms bins from -2.5 to +1.5 s, which overrides those analysis-specific parameters.\n- ALM photoinhibition occurs during the last 0.5 s of delay and ends before go cue. The supplied event start/stop timestamps are therefore the authoritative source for the required time-varying photostimulation input.\n- Video marker traces were obtained with DeepLabCut-style x/y/likelihood outputs. Likelihood is strongly bimodal in the NWBs. A confidence threshold is necessary before tongue y discretization; the exact threshold is not stated clearly in the supplied prose/code, so a conventional 0.9 threshold will be evaluated during mapping and visual validation.\n- The method paper commonly analyzes “regular” trials excluding early lick, auto/free-water, and stimulation when estimating unperturbed movement encoding. This decoder task explicitly requires early lick and photostimulation, so those valid classes must be retained.\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n- Kilosort2 clusters were characterized with 15 quality metrics.\n- Manual labels (`good`/`unlabelled`) from 28 penetrations trained five region-specific logistic-regression classifiers (cortex, striatum, thalamus, midbrain, medulla). The resulting classifier `good` units were used in the paper.\n- Reported classifier false-alarm rates: cortex 7.8%, striatum 6.4%, thalamus 7.3%, midbrain 5.5%, medulla 4.3%.\n- The QC white paper explicitly warns that thresholding individual metrics causes unacceptable misses/false alarms; therefore NWB `classification == good`, not `unit_quality`, is the reference-consistent filter.\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Trial exclusions in individual reference analyses are analysis-specific. For this task, trials with required labels and valid go cues are retained, including early-lick, photostimulation, auto-water, and free-water trials, unless neural/video validity prevents representation.\n- `is_good_trials` is a per-unit validity mask and must be respected. A fixed-neuron session matrix favors dropping classifier-good units that are invalid on any retained trial rather than silently filling neural data.\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n|------------------|----------|\n| Choice from behavior video, post-go | AUC ROC 0.99 ± 0.01 (106 sessions; not directly comparable to required neural decoder) |\n| Single-neuron choice selectivity | AUC > 0.65 used as classification threshold, not population decoder accuracy |\n\n### Paper/data discrepancy\n- Papers/white paper report 69,943 good units across 173 sessions. Supplied NWBs contain 69,453 `classification == good` units across 174 files, one of which has zero good units. Excluding that file reconciles the 173-session count but leaves a 490-unit difference, most plausibly a released-data/version or manuscript transposition difference. The conversion will use the supplied NWB classifier labels as authoritative and report the discrepancy rather than inventing units.\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Spike time reference | Author export says spikes already relative to go | NWB spikes/events are absolute session time | Analyses align to task epochs/go | Subtract each NWB trial go timestamp before binning, equivalently histogram absolute spikes with go-shifted edges. |\n| Neural binning | 40-ms sliding windows, 3.4-ms stride, -3 to +3 s | Raw spike times available | Method-paper processing matches code | Decoder specification overrides with 50-ms non-overlapping bins from -2.5 to +1.5 s (80 bins). |\n| QC unit count | Classifier mode is used | 69,453 classifier-good units; one file has zero | 69,943 good units / 173 sessions | Use supplied NWB labels. The AI first defensively truncates to `n = min(len(trials), len(go))`, then defines \"structurally valid\" trials as those rows that have an indexed go cue **and** at least one `sample_start_times` (tone) event at or before that go cue. Step 2: \"Go-cue checks in three sessions found exactly one go cue per trial, all within trial bounds.\" Step 4 resolves the sample/go mismatch: \"Sample events can outnumber completed trials … Latest sample start before each go yields one tone/trial.\" The trial table is used directly rather than re-deriving boundaries because it already encodes one row per behavioural trial with all required labels; the tone requirement exists because `input[0]` (time from tone onset) is undefined without one.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Four successive filters, all documented:\n1. **Structural**: must have a go cue and a preceding tone (see 1-d).\n2. **Ephys observation validity**: for every classifier-good unit, the AI reads that unit's `obs_intervals` and `is_good_trials`, maps each observation interval onto a behavioural trial row (candidate = latest trial starting before the interval end, accepted only if temporal overlap > 0), and keeps the **intersection** across all good units of trials that are both mapped and flagged `is_good_trials`. Results are cached per unique `obs_intervals` array. This removed 1,569 of 94,370 trials in 12 sessions.\n3. **All-zero neural windows**: after binning, any trial in which *no* selected unit emitted a single spike anywhere in the 4 s window is dropped as a recording gap. This removed 2,423 trials across 93 sessions.\n4. **Minimum session size**: a session with fewer than 2 surviving trials is dropped.\n\nNotably, the AI does **not** apply an explicit `free_water` exclusion, nor does it exclude early-lick / ignore / photostim / auto-water trials. Final: 90,378 trials retained.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef select_units_and_trials(nwb, structurally_valid_trials):\n    cls=arrcol(nwb.units,'classification').astype(str)\n    units=np.flatnonzero(cls=='good')\n    if units.size==0: return units, np.asarray([],dtype=int)\n    starts=arrcol(nwb.trials,'start_time',np.float64); stops=arrcol(nwb.trials,'stop_time',np.float64)\n    common=set(map(int,structurally_valid_trials)); map_cache={}\n    for j in units:\n        obs=np.asarray(nwb.units['obs_intervals'][j],dtype=np.float64)\n        valid=np.asarray(nwb.units['is_good_trials'][j],dtype=bool)\n        ...\n        mapped=np.searchsorted(starts,obs[:,1],side='left')-1\n        mapped=np.clip(mapped,0,len(starts)-1)\n        overlap=np.minimum(stops[mapped],obs[:,1])-np.maximum(starts[mapped],obs[:,0])\n        mapped=np.where(overlap>0,mapped,-1).astype(int)\n        common.intersection_update(map(int,mapped[valid & (mapped>=0)]))\n        if not common: break\n    return units, np.asarray(sorted(common),dtype=int)\n```\n```python\n        # Remove neural recording-gap trials: no selected unit emitted any\n        # spike anywhere in the requested four-second window.\n        neural_valid=np.any(rates>0,axis=(1,2))\n        dropped_zero_neural=int((~neural_valid).sum())\n        trial_idx=trial_idx[neural_valid]\n        go=go[neural_valid]; tone=tone[neural_valid]; rates=rates[neural_valid]\n        ...\n        if len(go)<2: return None\n```\n\niii. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. `nwb.units['spike_times']` (ragged, session-absolute seconds), restricted to units with `nwb.units['classification'] == 'good'`, together with `BehavioralEvents/go_start_times` timestamps which place the bin edges. `nwb.units['anno_name']` supplies the per-neuron brain-region label (stored verbatim as the full Allen CCF string, 293 unique labels).\n\nii.\n```python\n        cls=arrcol(nwb.units,'classification').astype(str)\n        units=np.flatnonzero(cls=='good')\n```\n```python\n        # Read each selected ragged spike vector once; histogramming below is in C.\n        spikes=[np.asarray(nwb.units['spike_times'][j],dtype=np.float64) for j in units]\n```\n```python\n        names=arrcol(nwb.units,'anno_name').astype(str)[units]\n        if np.any(names==''): raise ValueError(f'Blank anatomy among selected units: {path.name}')\n```\n\niii. Step 2: \"`nwbfile.units` contains spike times, waveform/spike-sorting metrics, `unit_quality`, classifier `classification`, Allen CCF `anno_name` …\". Spike times are the only neural representation in the file. Step 5 maps `units.spike_times → neural` via `sliding_histogram`-equivalent logic from the reference repo.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Per-bin spike **counts divided by the 50 ms bin width → firing rate in Hz**, float32. Implementation: build an `(n_trials, 81)` matrix of absolute bin edges, then for each unit do a single `np.searchsorted` over that whole matrix and `np.diff` along the edge axis, which yields counts per bin for all trials at once. No smoothing, no normalisation, no baseline subtraction, no z-scoring. Rates are stored as `(n_trials, n_units, n_time)` and sliced per trial into `(n_units, 80)`.\n\nii.\n```python\n        # Vectorize binning across all trials: one searchsorted call per unit.\n        # np.diff is along each trial's 81 edges, so no cross-trial bins are introduced.\n        edge_matrix=go[:,None]+EDGES_REL[None,:]\n        rates=np.empty((len(go),len(units),N_TIME),dtype=np.float32)\n        for ui,sp in enumerate(spikes):\n            rates[:,ui,:]=np.diff(np.searchsorted(sp,edge_matrix,side='left'),axis=1).astype(np.float32)/BIN_S\n```\n\niii. Step 5, decision 5: \"firing rates in Hz (count / 0.05 s), matching reference `rate=True`; float32 limits memory.\" The reference repo's `sliding_histogram(..., rate=True)` returns `binSpikes / bin_width`, so Hz is the reference-consistent representation. Step 10 records an independent check: \"Independent pynwb checks for three unit/trial firing-rate vectors matched conversion exactly (`np.allclose`, maximum difference 0).\"\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. A single unit-level filter: keep units whose `classification` column equals `'good'` — the verdict of the region-specific logistic-regression QC classifier described in the Chen/Liu et al. 2023 white paper. **No individual quality-metric thresholds** are applied, and `unit_quality` ('good'/'multi') is deliberately not used. A session with zero such units is dropped. Per-unit temporal validity (`is_good_trials`) is *not* used to drop units — instead it is pushed onto the trial axis (see 1-e), so that all classifier-good units survive. The AI also asserts that every retained unit has a non-empty anatomical label. Step 9 compares 69,453 retained units and 25.5% retention against the papers' 69,943 units / 25.9% of Kilosort2 clusters, and documents the 490-unit residual as a release/manuscript difference rather than inventing units.\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Alignment is to **go-cue onset**, using the `BehavioralEvents/go_start_times` timestamps. All NWB streams share one session-absolute clock, so no resampling or offset correction is needed: the fixed relative edge vector `EDGES_REL` (−2.5 … +1.5 s) is added to each trial's go-cue time to produce that trial's absolute edges, and spikes are searchsorted against those directly.\n\nii.\n```python\nBIN_S=0.050\nOFF_START=-2.5\nOFF_END=1.5\nEDGES_REL=np.arange(OFF_START, OFF_END + BIN_S/2, BIN_S, dtype=np.float64)\n```\n```python\n        edge_matrix=go[:,None]+EDGES_REL[None,:]\n```\n```python\n'temporal_alignment_event':'go cue onset','off_start':OFF_START,'off_end':OFF_END\n```\n\niii. Step 4 discrepancy table: \"Author export says spikes already relative to go / NWB spikes/events are absolute session time → Subtract each NWB trial go timestamp before binning, equivalently histogram absolute spikes with go-shifted edges.\" Step 10 check 2 independently re-histogrammed raw spike times from pynwb for selected unit/trial pairs and got exactly zero difference, confirming no off-by-one or offset error.\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. **50 ms non-overlapping bins**, 80 bins per trial spanning −2.5 s to +1.5 s around the go cue; bin centres at −2.475 … +1.475 s. The grid is built once at module level and reused for every trial and every session, so all trials share an identical time axis. No sliding window, no overlap, no post-hoc rebinning or downsampling; the binning is done once, directly from raw spike times. `metadata['time_bin_size'] = 50.0` (ms) and the bin centres are stored in metadata. The AI explicitly departs from the method paper's 40 ms window / 3.4 ms stride over −3 … +3 s.\n\nii.\n```python\nBIN_S=0.050\nEDGES_REL=np.arange(OFF_START, OFF_END + BIN_S/2, BIN_S, dtype=np.float64)\nCENTERS_REL=(EDGES_REL[:-1]+EDGES_REL[1:])/2\nN_TIME=len(CENTERS_REL)\n```\n```python\n    assert N_TIME==80 and len(EDGES_REL)==81\n```\n```python\n'time_bin_size':50.0,'time_bin_units':'ms', ... 'n_timepoints':N_TIME,'bin_centers_seconds':CENTERS_REL.astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. Step 4: \"Neural binning — 40-ms sliding windows, 3.4-ms stride, −3 to +3 s … Decoder specification overrides with 50-ms non-overlapping bins from −2.5 to +1.5 s (80 bins).\" The AI treats this as one of the sanctioned discrepancies (\"required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications\"). The `OFF_END + BIN_S/2` term in `arange` is a guard against floating-point edge loss, and the `N_TIME==80 and len(EDGES_REL)==81` assertion in `validate()` enforces it.\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds* derived from?\n\ni. `BehavioralEvents/sample_start_times` (the auditory sample-epoch/tone onsets) together with `BehavioralEvents/go_start_times`. For each trial the AI takes the **last tone at or before that trial's go cue** (`searchsorted(..., side='right') - 1`), which handles the fact that a lick during the sample epoch replays that epoch and produces multiple tone events per trial.\n\nii.\n```python\n        sample=np.asarray(ev['sample_start_times'].timestamps[:],dtype=np.float64)\n        n=min(len(tr),len(go))\n        ix=np.searchsorted(sample,go[:n],side='right')-1\n        structural_idx=np.flatnonzero(ix>=0)\n        ...\n        go=go[trial_idx]; tone=sample[ix[trial_idx]]\n```\n\niii. Step 4: \"Sample events can outnumber completed trials / Latest sample start before each go yields one tone/trial; median tone-to-go 1.85 s → Pair each go with latest preceding sample start; variable delays are preserved in elapsed-time input.\" The 1.85 s median matches the task structure (0.65 s sample + 1.2 s delay).\n\n## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds*?\n\ni. A continuous, time-varying value per bin: the **absolute time of the bin centre minus the trial's tone onset**, in seconds, cast to float32. It is written into row 0 of the `(2, 80)` input matrix. Because the bins are go-aligned and the tone-to-go interval varies by trial, the vector is a ramp whose offset encodes the delay length. Values span [−1.5, 11.9] s over the full dataset (negative early in the window when the tone had not yet occurred; the long tail comes from rare trials with repeated sample replays).\n\nii.\n```python\n            centers=g+CENTERS_REL\n            inp=np.empty((2,N_TIME),dtype=np.float32)\n            inp[0]=(centers-to).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. Step 5, decision 8: \"elapsed time from tone onset is represented continuously as explicitly required, despite the generic format note suggesting binary onset series for onset-only inputs.\" The AI read the Decoder Task spec as unambiguous (\"Time from tone onset in seconds (continuous, time-varying)\") and let it override the generic \"represent onset times as a binary time series\" formatting note. Step 10 verified the vector against an independent event-pairing computation with `np.allclose` (max diff < 2.3e-7, float32 rounding only).\n\n## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. It is evaluated on exactly the same grid used to bin the spikes: `centers = go + CENTERS_REL`, where `CENTERS_REL` is the midpoint vector of the same `EDGES_REL` array used to build the spike-count edges. So bin *k* of the input covers the same interval as bin *k* of the firing rates by construction, with no interpolation and no separate clock.\n\nii.\n```python\nCENTERS_REL=(EDGES_REL[:-1]+EDGES_REL[1:])/2\n```\n```python\n        edge_matrix=go[:,None]+EDGES_REL[None,:]      # neural\n...\n            centers=g+CENTERS_REL                      # inputs/outputs\n```\n\niii. Step 5, decision 4: \"80 bins with edges [−2.5, 1.5] and centers [−2.475, 1.475] relative to go; metadata offsets describe edges.\" Step 12 check 5: \"direct raw spike histograms and event-derived inputs passed 108 independent checks.\"\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Photostimulation* derived from?\n\ni. The `BehavioralEvents/photostim_start_times` and `photostim_stop_times` **event timestamp streams** (session-absolute), not the `trials` table's string-valued `photostim_onset` / `photostim_duration` columns. These two streams are equal-length and index-paired (I verified in `sub-440956_ses-20190207`: 78 starts, 78 stops, all durations 0.5 s, and the start timestamps are numerically identical to `trials.start_time + float(photostim_onset)`).\n\nii.\n```python\n        pstart=np.asarray(ev['photostim_start_times'].timestamps[:],dtype=np.float64)\n        pstop=np.asarray(ev['photostim_stop_times'].timestamps[:],dtype=np.float64)\n```\n\niii. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Photostimulation*?\n\ni. A **binary (0/1) time-varying series**, one value per bin, stored as float32 in row 1 of the input matrix. For each bin centre the AI finds the latest stimulation interval starting at or before it (`searchsorted(pstart, centers, 'right') - 1`) and sets 1 if the centre is still before that interval's stop — i.e. membership in a half-open `[start, stop)` interval. Sessions with no photostim events get an all-zero row. Full-dataset input range is [0, 1] as verified.\n\nii.\n```python\n            if len(pstart):\n                # true at center if any half-open stimulation interval contains center\n                jj=np.searchsorted(pstart,centers,side='right')-1\n                valid=jj>=0; stim=np.zeros(N_TIME,dtype=bool)\n                stim[valid]=centers[valid] < pstop[jj[valid]]\n                inp[1]=stim.astype(np.float32)\n            else: inp[1]=0\n```\n```python\n    assert set(np.unique(i[1])).issubset({0.,1.})\n```\n\niii. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Tongue y-position* derived from?\n\ni. `acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries/Camera0_side_TongueTracking`, a DeepLabCut-style `(n_frames, 3)` array `= (tongue_x, tongue_y, tongue_likelihood)` with matching `timestamps` at ~3.4 ms (~294 Hz). Column 1 is the y value, column 2 the tracking likelihood. The AI selects `Camera0_side_TongueTracking` by name when present and otherwise falls back deterministically to the lexicographically first series whose name contains `TongueTracking`; if there is none, it returns all-NaN thresholds so every bin becomes \"not visible\".\n\nii.\n```python\ndef get_tongue(nwb):\n    series=nwb.acquisition['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series\n    keys=sorted(k for k in series if 'TongueTracking' in k)\n    if not keys:\n        return None,None,None,None,(np.nan,np.nan)\n    key='Camera0_side_TongueTracking' if 'Camera0_side_TongueTracking' in keys else keys[0]\n    ts=series[key]\n    times=np.asarray(ts.timestamps[:],dtype=np.float64)\n    data=np.asarray(ts.data[:],dtype=np.float32)\n    y=data[:,1]; likelihood=data[:,2]\n```\n\niii. Step 2: \"`BehavioralTimeSeries` contains side-camera jaw, nose, and tongue x/y/likelihood traces, normally at 3.4-ms sampling. Some sessions additionally contain lick-port tracking or a second camera.\" Step 5: \"Prefer Camera0 for consistency; fall back deterministically to lexicographically first tongue stream only if Camera0 absent.\"\n\n## 8-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Tongue y-position*?\n\ni. Three steps. (1) **Visibility gating**: a frame counts only if `y` and `likelihood` are finite and `likelihood >= 0.90`; the tracker emits a position even when the tongue is retracted, so low-confidence frames must not be treated as measurements. (2) **Session thresholds**: the 40th and 60th percentiles of `y` are computed over *all visible frames in the whole session* (raw frames, not bin averages). (3) **Per-bin sampling**: for each go-aligned bin centre the AI takes the **nearest video frame** (not a within-bin average), requiring that the centre lie inside the video coverage span *and* that the nearest frame be within 10 ms; the frame's y is then thresholded. Bins with no covered/confident frame get class 3. Full-dataset result: 0.062 / 0.032 / 0.065 / 0.841 — i.e. ~15.9 % of bins visible, split ~39 / 20 / 41 % within the visible fraction.\n\nii.\n```python\n    visible=np.isfinite(y)&np.isfinite(likelihood)&(likelihood>=LIKELIHOOD_THRESHOLD)\n    thresholds=tuple(np.percentile(y[visible],[40,60]).tolist()) if visible.any() else (np.nan,np.nan)\n```\n```python\n            tc=np.full(N_TIME,3,dtype=np.int64); vis=np.zeros(N_TIME,dtype=bool)\n            if vt is not None and len(vt):\n                q=nearest_indices(vt,centers)\n                # Do not extrapolate beyond video coverage; nearest frame must also be temporally close.\n                covered=(centers>=vt[0])&(centers<=vt[-1])&(np.abs(vt[q]-centers)<=0.010)\n                vis=covered&np.isfinite(vy[q])&np.isfinite(vl[q])&(vl[q]>=LIKELIHOOD_THRESHOLD)&np.isfinite(p40)&np.isfinite(p60)\n```\n\niii. Step 4: \"Likelihood is strongly bimodal; 13 sessions have incomplete video coverage → Use likelihood >= 0.9 as visible; uncovered or low-confidence bins are class 3. Compute session percentiles only from visible y samples. Threshold choice has negligible impact because confidence is near 0 or 1 and will be plot-validated.\" Step 3 notes the threshold \"is not stated clearly in the supplied prose/code, so a conventional 0.9 threshold will be evaluated during mapping and visual validation.\" Step 7: \"High not-visible frequency is expected because tongue confidence is high primarily during licking.\"\n\n## 8-c. How is `output` *Tongue y-position* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Four classes, exactly as specified: `0` below the session 40th percentile, `1` between the 40th and 60th percentiles **inclusive at both edges**, `2` above the 60th percentile, `3` not visible. Percentiles are per-session and computed from visible frames only. Equality at a boundary is assigned to the middle class. `output_values[3] = ['<40th percentile','40th to 60th percentile','>60th percentile','not visible']`. The p40/p60 values are stored per session in `metadata['session_info']` for auditability.\n\nii.\n```python\nLIKELIHOOD_THRESHOLD=0.90\n```\n```python\n                yy=vy[q]; tc[vis & (yy<p40)]=0; tc[vis & (yy>=p40)&(yy<=p60)]=1; tc[vis & (yy>p60)]=2\n            out[3]=tc\n```\n```python\n'output_values':[..., ['<40th percentile','40th to 60th percentile','>60th percentile','not visible']],\n'tongue_discretization':'Per-session visible-frame y percentiles: <p40, p40-p60, >p60; low-confidence/uncovered=not visible'\n```\n\niii. Step 5, decision 7: \"compute p40/p60 from all confidence-valid y frames across the session, as 'over the session' requires. Equality is assigned to the middle class.\" The `--show-processing` plots draw the raw y trace, the likelihood trace, the p40/p60 lines and the resulting class series on one go-relative axis, plus the session class-fraction histogram, specifically to make the discretisation visually checkable.\n\n## 8-d. How is `output` *Tongue y-position* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Camera timestamps share the same session-absolute clock as spikes and events, so alignment is by nearest-neighbour resampling of the video onto the identical go-aligned bin-centre grid used for the firing rates: `centers = go + CENTERS_REL`, `q = nearest_indices(vt, centers)`. Two guards prevent silent extrapolation: the centre must lie within `[vt[0], vt[-1]]`, and the matched frame must be within 10 ms of the centre (comfortably satisfied at 3.4 ms frame spacing, ≤1.7 ms worst case). Anything outside coverage — including the leading bins of trials where the video is trial-gated and starts after `go − 2.5 s` — becomes class 3.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef nearest_indices(sorted_times, query):\n    \"\"\"Nearest indices in monotonically increasing sorted_times.\"\"\"\n    j=np.searchsorted(sorted_times, query, side='left')\n    j=np.clip(j, 0, len(sorted_times)-1)\n    prev=np.maximum(j-1,0)\n    use_prev=np.abs(query-sorted_times[prev]) <= np.abs(sorted_times[j]-query)\n    return np.where(use_prev,prev,j)\n```\n```python\n                q=nearest_indices(vt,centers)\n                covered=(centers>=vt[0])&(centers<=vt[-1])&(np.abs(vt[q]-centers)<=0.010)\n```\n\niii. Step 5 mapping table: \"Resample nearest video frame to each bin center; if no coverage or likelihood<0.9 → 3.\" Step 4: \"13 sessions have incomplete tongue-video coverage, so uncovered time bins should be labeled 'not visible', not used for percentile thresholds.\" Step 10 check 2 independently re-derived three trials' tongue vectors straight from pynwb and compared with `np.allclose`; all passed.\n\n## 9. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Six cases, each with an explicit policy:\n- **Session never quality-controlled** (`classification` is NaN for all 1,852 units in `sub-440958_ses-20190216T162508`): `.astype(str)` turns NaN into `'nan'`, no unit matches `'good'`, and the session is dropped. This is what reconciles 174 files with the papers' 173 sessions.\n- **Trials with no ephys observation / flagged bad by `is_good_trials`**: removed via the obs-interval→trial mapping intersection (1,569 trials).\n- **Trials with a recording gap** (no spike from any unit in the whole 4 s window): removed after binning (2,423 trials).\n- **Trials with no preceding tone event**: removed by the `ix >= 0` structural filter.\n- **Missing/short video coverage or low-confidence tracking**: mapped to the explicit \"not visible\" class 3 rather than imputed or dropped, so otherwise valid neural trials are kept.\n- **Sessions with no photostim events**: the photostim input row is set to all zeros rather than erroring.\n- Additionally, unexpected label strings (`outcome`, `early_lick`) raise rather than being silently coerced, and a blank `anno_name` on a selected unit raises `ValueError` (never triggered — all 69,453 good units are labelled).\n\nii.\n```python\n    cls=arrcol(nwb.units,'classification').astype(str)\n    units=np.flatnonzero(cls=='good')\n    if units.size==0: return units, np.asarray([],dtype=int)\n```\n```python\n        if np.any(names==''): raise ValueError(f'Blank anatomy among selected units: {path.name}')\n```\n```python\n            else: inp[1]=0\n```\n```python\n            tc=np.full(N_TIME,3,dtype=np.int64); vis=np.zeros(N_TIME,dtype=bool)\n            if vt is not None and len(vt):\n```\n```python\ndef validate(data):\n    ...\n            assert np.isfinite(n).all() and np.isfinite(i).all() and (n>=0).all()\n```\n\niii. The governing principle in Step 5/Step 9: where a measurement was genuinely never recorded, the session or trial is excluded rather than emitted as fabricated zeros (\"Direct raw checks confirmed correct go alignment and zero spikes across all selected units; these invalid trials were removed\"); where the measurement legitimately has no value (retracted tongue, video not rolling), it is represented as an explicit fourth category, because \"missing video coverage maps to the mandated not-visible class rather than dropping otherwise valid neural trials.\" The AI also chose to *keep* all classifier-good units and push per-unit invalidity onto the trial axis so that no neural values are imputed and neuron dimensions stay fixed.\n\n## 10-a. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n\ni. The full conversion runs 209 s for 174 files (~1.2 s/session, range 0.59–2.38 s), and pickling the 12.0 GB result adds tens of seconds. The dominant costs are HDF5 I/O and per-unit work, both scaling with unit count: (1) `select_units_and_trials` issues **two HDF5 reads per classifier-good unit** (`obs_intervals[j]`, `is_good_trials[j]`) — up to ~1,800 reads per session — and only caches the *mapping* after the read, not the read itself; (2) `spikes=[np.asarray(nwb.units['spike_times'][j]) ...]` performs one ragged `VectorIndex.__getitem__` per unit rather than reading the underlying buffer once; (3) the per-unit `np.searchsorted` over the `(n_trials, 81)` edge matrix; (4) loading the full `(n_frames, 3)` tongue array (~680 k × 3). The AI did not profile at this granularity, but it did identify and eliminate the original dominant cost.\n\nii.\n```python\n    for j in units:\n        obs=np.asarray(nwb.units['obs_intervals'][j],dtype=np.float64)\n        valid=np.asarray(nwb.units['is_good_trials'][j],dtype=bool)\n```\n```python\n        spikes=[np.asarray(nwb.units['spike_times'][j],dtype=np.float64) for j in units]\n        edge_matrix=go[:,None]+EDGES_REL[None,:]\n        for ui,sp in enumerate(spikes):\n            rates[:,ui,:]=np.diff(np.searchsorted(sp,edge_matrix,side='left'),axis=1).astype(np.float32)/BIN_S\n```\n```python\n        st=time.time(); res=convert_session(p,...)\n        ... print(f\"[{len(sessions)}] {p.name}: ... {time.time()-st:.2f}s\",flush=True)\n```\n\niii. Step 6/Step 7: \"Initial implementation uses a per-unit/per-trial `np.histogram`; sample timing will determine whether vectorized session-wide binning is required\" → \"Vectorized all-trial binning with one `searchsorted` per unit — Sample reduced from 27.4 s to 2.8 s (~9.8x).\" Step 10: \"Runtime bottleneck: per-unit/per-trial histograms projected >15 minutes. Replaced with one vectorized `searchsorted` operation per unit, yielding a final full runtime of 209 s.\" The AI also added obs-mapping caching after an interrupted slow dry scan, and reports per-session timing to stdout for bottleneck identification. Runtime is comfortably inside the instructions' 15-minute budget.\n\n## 10-b. What loops in the code could have been vectorized to improve efficiency?\n\ni. Three remain:\n1. **`for k,(g,to) in enumerate(zip(go,tone))`** — the per-trial loop that builds inputs and outputs. All of its contents are trivially broadcastable across the trial axis: `inp[0]` is `CENTERS_REL[None,:] + (go-tone)[:,None]`; the photostim `searchsorted` could be done once on a flattened `(n_trials*80,)` centre array; the static outputs are a broadcast of three per-trial vectors; and the tongue `nearest_indices` call could likewise run once over all centres. This is the most vectorizable remaining loop and it re-enters `np.searchsorted`/`nearest_indices` ~90,000 times over the dataset.\n2. **`for j in units`** in `select_units_and_trials` — necessarily per-unit because of ragged HDF5 storage, but the intersection could at least short-circuit on identical `obs_intervals` *before* reading, and could operate on boolean masks instead of Python `set` operations.\n3. **`for ui,sp in enumerate(spikes)`** — genuinely irreducible, since each unit has a differently sized sorted spike array; the trial dimension is already vectorised by the `(n_trials, 81)` edge matrix.\n\nii.\n```python\n        for k,(g,to) in enumerate(zip(go,tone)):\n            fr=rates[k]\n            centers=g+CENTERS_REL\n            inp=np.empty((2,N_TIME),dtype=np.float32)\n            inp[0]=(centers-to).astype(np.float32)\n            if len(pstart):\n                jj=np.searchsorted(pstart,centers,side='right')-1\n                ...\n            q=nearest_indices(vt,centers)\n            ...\n            neural.append(fr); inputs.append(inp); outputs.append(out); tongue_visible.append(vis)\n```\n\niii. The AI's own efficiency note is limited to the binning loop it already fixed (\"Reads each retained unit spike vector once per session; uses float32 for neural/input matrices and avoids full Units DataFrame materialization\"). It did not flag the per-trial input/output loop, presumably because the measured 209 s runtime already met the 15-minute budget, so there was no forcing function to optimise further.\n\n## 10-c. What processing does the code repeat multiple times?\n\ni. Four repetitions, all minor:\n1. **Per-unit HDF5 reads of `obs_intervals` / `is_good_trials`** in `select_units_and_trials`. The `map_cache` keyed on `(obs.shape, obs.tobytes())` avoids recomputing the *mapping*, but the read and the `tobytes()` hash still happen for every one of the hundreds of good units, and in most sessions all units share one identical interval array.\n2. **Per-trial `np.searchsorted(pstart, centers, ...)`** and **`nearest_indices(vt, centers)`** — the same global `pstart`/`vt` arrays are searched once per trial instead of once per session.\n3. **A second full traversal in `validate()`**, which re-walks every trial of every session checking shapes and finiteness after the arrays were already built.\n4. **Firing rates are computed for trials that are then discarded** by the `neural_valid` filter (2,423 of 92,801 trials, ~2.6% of the binning work).\n\nNothing is recomputed across sessions: each file is opened exactly once, the tongue array and percentiles are computed once per session, and the bin grid is built once at module scope.\n\nii.\n```python\n        key=(obs.shape,obs.tobytes())\n        mapped=map_cache.get(key)\n        if mapped is None:\n            ...\n            map_cache[key]=mapped\n```\n```python\n            if len(pstart):\n                jj=np.searchsorted(pstart,centers,side='right')-1\n            ...\n                q=nearest_indices(vt,centers)\n```\n```python\ndef validate(data):\n    ...\n    for s in range(ns):\n        for n,i,o in zip(data['neural'][s],data['input'][s],data['output'][s]):\n```\n\niii. The AI documented the caching decision explicitly in Step 13: \"Observation mappings are cached by unique interval arrays for efficiency.\" `validate()` is a deliberate correctness cost, not an oversight — Step 6 lists \"assertions\" as part of the design, and the instructions require validating shapes and types at each step. The rate-then-drop ordering is a consequence of the all-zero criterion being defined on the binned rates themselves.\n\n## 10-d. What unnecessary processing does the code do that is discarded in downstream analyses?\n\ni. A small amount, none of it on the critical path:\n1. **`tongue_visible`** is built for every trial of every session but is only ever consumed inside the `make_plot` branch, so it is pure waste on the 171 non-plotted sessions.\n2. **`candidates=units`** is a redundant alias kept only so `info` can report `classifier_good_units` alongside `retained_units` — the two are always equal under the final policy (the AI's earlier plan to drop drift-affected units was abandoned).\n3. **Firing rates for the 2,423 trials later dropped** as recording gaps are computed and then thrown away.\n4. **Output arrays are `int64`** when the values are all in 0–3; `int8` would be 8× smaller (≈230 MB → ≈29 MB). Trials are the rows of the NWB `trials` table, paired one-to-one with the `go_start_times` event stream. The AI first defensively truncates to `n = min(len(trials), len(go))`, then defines \"structurally valid\" trials as those rows that have an indexed go cue **and** at least one `sample_start_times` (tone) event at or before that go cue.
